DESIGN.md

show
## Concept
The player controls a constantly orbiting dot that can instantly toggle between a tight inner orbit and a wide outer orbit, using each tap to weave through obstacles placed on either track.

## Player
A small, glowing dot that revolves around a fixed central point. The player’s only verb is **toggle orbit** (tap or click) which switches the dot from the inner path to the outer path and back.

## Core loop
The dot spins continuously while obstacles appear on the inner and outer tracks. Every few seconds a new obstacle spawns, giving the player a brief window to decide whether to stay in the current orbit or toggle to the opposite radius. The player watches the upcoming pattern, taps at the right moment, and the dot instantly flips its path, avoiding collisions and earning points.

## Hazards / Obstacles
- **Static protrusions** that extend from the inner or outer track, requiring the player to be on the opposite radius to pass.
- **Sweeping arcs** that travel along one orbit, temporarily blocking that radius and forcing a toggle.
- **Radial projectiles** that cross the space between the two orbits, threatening the dot if it happens to be in the crossing zone.
Each hazard has a clear visual cue that telegraphs its motion, allowing the player to time their toggle.

## Score
Score increases each time the player successfully passes an obstacle without colliding, and extra points are awarded for rapid toggles that avoid a dangerous sweep.

## Win and lose
The player loses immediately on collision with any obstacle, triggering a Game Over overlay that shows the final score and a prompt to restart. The player wins after surviving a pre‑defined wave of obstacles (e.g., after a set number of cycles), prompting a victory screen with celebratory graphics and the final score.

## Feel
A fast‑paced, rhythm‑driven experience with a neon‑glow aesthetic. The orbiting motion feels steady and hypnotic, while the toggle action injects bursts of urgency. Sound consists of crisp beeps for toggles and short bursts for obstacle interactions, reinforcing the kinetic feel.

## Why it's fun
The thrill of a split‑second tap that flips the dot out of a collision path, turning a near‑miss into a triumphant escape.

TASKS.md

show
1. Setup HTML scaffold & meta.json — create index.html with header, canvas, restart button and matching meta.json per CONVENTIONS.md.
2. Initialize game state objects — define player, hazards array, score, phase, and other constants at top of script.
3. Implement main loop with requestAnimationFrame — startLoop function with cancelAnimationFrame, error handling, clear canvas each frame.
4. Add input handling — keys Set for keydown/keyup, R key to restart, restart button click to call reset().
5. Implement player orbit toggling movement — dt-based physics for orbit radius toggle, ensure controls affect player position.
6. Create hazard data structures and spawn logic — ensure SAFE_RADIUS safety and motion scaling with dt.
7. Add collision detection — detect player vs hazards, switch state to "gameover" and stop gameplay.
8. Implement scoring system — increase score on successful hazard passes, render score on canvas top-left.
9. Render all elements — draw background, hazards, player, HUD; ensure player high contrast.
10. Add game‑over overlay — dim background, display "Game Over", final score, and restart instructions.
11. Expose observable contract — populate window.__game each frame with state, score, player position/visibility, objective.
12. Integrate audio cues — lazy‑init AudioContext, beep() helper, trigger sounds on toggle, score, hit, death.

<body>
  <header class="game-header">
    <h1>Orbit Radius</h1>
    <p class="game-description">A dot constantly orbits a central point; each tap toggles between a tight inner orbit and a wide outer orbit, letting you navigate obstacles placed at either radius.</p>
    <ul class="game-controls">
      <li>Tap or click to toggle orbit radius</li>
      <li>Press R or click Restart</li>
    </ul>
  </header>
  <main>
    <canvas id="game" width="800" height="600"></canvas>
    <button id="restart" class="game-restart" type="button">Restart</button>
  </main>
  <script>
    // === CONFIG =============================================================
    const W = 800, H = 600;

    // === STATE ==============================================================
    const canvas = document.getElementById('game');
    const ctx = canvas.getContext('2d');

    let lastTime = performance.now();
    let loopId = null;

    // === LOOP/UPDATE =========================================================
    function startLoop() {
      if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
      function tick(now) {
        try {
          const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
          lastTime = now;
          // Clear canvas each frame
          ctx.clearRect(0, 0, W, H);
          // Future update/render logic will go here
        } catch (err) {
          console.error('loop error', err);
        }
        loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
      }
      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }

    // Start the loop immediately
    startLoop();
  </script>
</body>
</html>

We'll create sections:

// === CONFIG =============================================================

(W, H constants etc)

const CENTER_X = W / 2;
const CENTER_Y = H / 2;
const PLAYER_RADIUS = 6;
const PLAYER_ORBIT_INNER = 80;
const PLAYER_ORBIT_OUTER = 200;
const PLAYER_SPEED = Math.PI * 0.5; // rad per second, half rotation per sec? use 1.2 rad maybe
const HAZARD_RADIUS = 8;
const HAZARD_SPAWN_INTERVAL = 1.5; // seconds
const SAFE_ANGLE = Math.PI / 8; // offset ensure safe.

const OBJECTIVE_TEXT = "Tap to toggle orbit radius, avoid obstacles.";

... plus audio beep perhaps later.

Now // === STATE =============================================================

canvas, ctx, let player = { angle: 0, radius: PLAYER_ORBIT_INNER, x:0, y:0 };
let hazards = [];
let score = 0;
let phase = "menu"; // start menu? We'll directly start playing after reset.
let lastTime = performance.now();
let loopId = null;
let keys = new Set();
let toggleRequested = false;
let nextHazardSpawn = 0;
let audioCtx = null;

let prevAngle = 0;

Now // === RESET =============================================================

function reset() {
    player.angle = 0;
    player.radius = PLAYER_ORBIT_INNER;
    player.x = CENTER_X + Math.cos(player.angle) * player.radius;
    player.y = CENTER_Y + Math.sin(player.angle) * player.radius;
    hazards = [];
    score = 0;
    phase = "playing";
    lastTime = performance.now();
    nextHazardSpawn = HAZARD_SPAWN_INTERVAL;
    toggleRequested = false;
    prevAngle = 0;
    publishState();
    startLoop();
}

Now // === LOOP/UPDATE =========================================================

function startLoop() {
  if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
  function tick(now) {
    try {
      const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
      lastTime = now;
      update(dt);
      render();
      publishState();
    } catch (err) {
      console.error('loop error', err);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }
  loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
}

function update(dt) {
  if (phase !== "playing") return;
  // Handle toggle
  if (toggleRequested) {
    player.radius = (player.radius === PLAYER_ORBIT_INNER) ? PLAYER_ORBIT_OUTER : PLAYER_ORBIT_INNER;
    toggleRequested = false;
    beep(440, 0.1);
  }
  // Update angle
  player.angle += PLAYER_SPEED * dt;
  // wrap angle
  if (player.angle > Math.PI * 2) player.angle -= Math.PI * 2;
  // compute position
  player.x = CENTER_X + Math.cos(player.angle) * player.radius;
  player.y = CENTER_Y + Math.sin(player.angle) * player.radius;
  // Score on full rotation
  if (prevAngle > player.angle) {
    score += 1;
    beep(660, 0.05);
  }
  prevAngle = player.angle;
  // Spawn hazards
  nextHazardSpawn -= dt;
  if (nextHazardSpawn <= 0) {
    spawnHazard();
    nextHazardSpawn = HAZARD_SPAWN_INTERVAL;
  }
  // Update hazards (if moving, but static)
  // Collision detection
  for (let i = hazards.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    const h = hazards[i];
    const hx = CENTER_X + Math.cos(h.angle) * h.radius;
    const hy = CENTER_Y + Math.sin(h.angle) * h.radius;
    const dist = Math.hypot(player.x - hx, player.y - hy);
    if (dist < PLAYER_RADIUS + HAZARD_RADIUS) {
      phase = "gameover";
      beep(220, 0.3);
      break;
    }
  }
  // Remove hazards that are behind player? Not necessary for simplicity.
}

function spawnHazard() {
  // Random radius inner/outer
  const radius = Math.random() < 0.5 ? PLAYER_ORBIT_INNER : PLAYER_ORBIT_OUTER;
  // Choose angle not too close to player's current angle
  let angle;
  let attempts = 0;
  do {
    angle = Math.random() * Math.PI * 2;
    const diff = Math.abs(angle - player.angle);
    const delta = Math.min(diff, Math.PI * 2 - diff);
    attempts++;
    if (attempts > 30) break;
  } while (Math.abs(angle - player.angle) < SAFE_ANGLE);
  hazards.push({ angle, radius });
}

Now // === RENDER =============================================================

function render() {
  // Already cleared in tick
  // Draw central point
  ctx.fillStyle = "#7df9ff";
  ctx.beginPath();
  ctx.arc(CENTER_X, CENTER_Y, 4, 0, Math.PI * 2);
  ctx.fill();
  // Draw hazards
  ctx.fillStyle = "#ff5252";
  for (const h of hazards) {
    const hx = CENTER_X + Math.cos(h.angle) * h.radius;
    const hy = CENTER_Y + Math.sin(h.angle) * h.radius;
    ctx.beginPath();
    ctx.arc(hx, hy, HAZARD_RADIUS, 0, Math.PI * 2);
    ctx.fill();
  }
  // Draw player
  ctx.fillStyle = "#ffd23f";
  ctx.beginPath();
  ctx.arc(player.x, player.y, PLAYER_RADIUS, 0, Math.PI * 2);
  ctx.fill();
  // HUD
  ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
  ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
  ctx.textAlign = "left";
  ctx.fillText(`Score ${score}`, 12, 22);
  // Game over overlay
  if (phase === "gameover") {
    ctx.fillStyle = "rgba(5,5,12,0.65)";
    ctx.fillRect(0, 0, W, H);
    ctx.textAlign = "center";
    ctx.font = "44px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
    ctx.fillStyle = "#ff52ae";
    ctx.fillText("Game Over", W / 2, H / 2 - 20);
    ctx.font = "20px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
    ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
    ctx.fillText(`Final score ${score}`, W / 2, H / 2 + 14);
    ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
    ctx.fillStyle = "#a5a5b8";
    ctx.fillText("Press R or click Restart", W / 2, H / 2 + 42);
  }
}

document.addEventListener("keydown", (ev) => {
  if (ev.key === "r" || ev.key === "R") { reset(); return; }
  keys.add(ev.key);
});

document.addEventListener("keyup", (ev) => {
  keys.delete(ev.key);
});

canvas.addEventListener("click", (ev) => {
  toggleRequested = true;
});

restartBtn.addEventListener("click", reset);

Now // === AUDIO (lazy) =======================================================

function beep(freq, durSec) {
  try {
    if (!audioCtx) audioCtx = new (window.AudioContext || window.webkitAudioContext)();
    const osc = audioCtx.createOscillator();
    const gain = audioCtx.createGain();
    osc.type = "triangle";
    osc.frequency.value = freq;
    gain.gain.setValueAtTime(0.04, audioCtx.currentTime);
    gain.gain.exponentialRampToValueAtTime(0.001, audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
    osc.connect(gain);
    gain.connect(audioCtx.destination);
    osc.start();
    osc.stop(audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
  } catch {}
}

Now // === OBSERVABLE CONTRACT ================================================

function publishState() {
  window.__game = {
    state: phase,
    score,
    player: {
      x: player.x,
      y: player.y,
      visible: player.x >= 0 && player.x < W && player.y >= 0 && player.y < H,
    },
    objective: OBJECTIVE_TEXT,
  };
}

prompt (15,910 chars)
## Reference example: "asteroids"

The following is a complete, validator-passing game. **Use it as a structural template** — your output must follow the same section ordering, the same patterns for the loop / restart / observable contract, and the same overall code style. Do NOT reproduce this game; you are building a different game described in the prompt below. The example exists only to anchor structure.

Notice in particular:
- The `// === SECTION ===` divider banners and their fixed order: CONFIG → STATE → RESET → LOOP/UPDATE → RENDER → INPUT → AUDIO → OBSERVABLE CONTRACT → BOOT.
- Plain object literals for game state (no `class`).
- A `keys` Set polled each frame instead of mutating game state from key handlers.
- `if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` at the top of `startLoop()`.
- The animation loop body wrapped in `try/catch`.
- `publishState()` called once per frame at the end of the tick.
- `window.__game` populated honestly (player.visible reflects actual viewport position).
- AudioContext initialized lazily inside `beep()`, wrapped in try/catch.

### Example `meta.json`
```json
{
  "title": "Neon Asteroids",
  "description": "Rotate, thrust, and shoot. Big rocks split into small ones. Get hit and you're gone.",
  "controls": [
    "Left/Right (or A/D) to rotate",
    "Up (or W) to thrust",
    "Space to fire",
    "Press R or click Restart"
  ],
  "tags": ["arcade", "shooter", "physics"]
}
```

### Example `index.html`
```html
<!DOCTYPE html>
<html lang="en">
<head>
  <meta charset="utf-8">
  <title>Neon Asteroids</title>
  <style>
    body { margin: 0; background: #0a0a0f; color: #e6e6f0; font-family: ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif; }
    .game-header { padding: 12px 16px; border-bottom: 1px solid #1d1d2a; }
    .game-header h1 { margin: 0; font-size: 18px; color: #7df9ff; }
    .game-header .game-description { margin: 4px 0; color: #a5a5b8; }
    .game-header .game-controls { margin: 4px 0; padding-left: 18px; color: #c8c8d8; }
    main { display: flex; flex-direction: column; align-items: center; padding: 16px; }
    canvas { background: #05050c; border: 1px solid #2a2f44; }
    .game-restart { margin-top: 10px; padding: 8px 14px; background: #1c2030; color: #7df9ff; border: 1px solid #2a2f44; cursor: pointer; }
    .game-restart:hover { background: #232842; }
  </style>
</head>
<body>
  <header class="game-header">
    <h1>Neon Asteroids</h1>
    <p class="game-description">Rotate, thrust, and shoot. Big rocks split into small ones. Get hit and you're gone.</p>
    <ul class="game-controls">
      <li>Left/Right (or A/D) to rotate</li>
      <li>Up (or W) to thrust</li>
      <li>Space to fire</li>
      <li>Press R or click Restart</li>
    </ul>
  </header>
  <main>
    <canvas id="game" width="800" height="600"></canvas>
    <button id="restart" class="game-restart" type="button">Restart</button>
  </main>
  <script>
  // === CONFIG =============================================================
  const W = 800, H = 600;
  const SHIP_RADIUS = 12;
  const SHIP_THRUST = 280;       // px/sec^2
  const SHIP_FRICTION = 0.6;     // velocity decay per second (1 = none)
  const SHIP_TURN = Math.PI * 1.8; // rad/sec
  const BULLET_SPEED = 520;      // px/sec
  const BULLET_LIFE = 1.0;       // sec
  const FIRE_COOLDOWN = 0.20;    // sec between shots
  const ASTEROID_BIG_R = 38;
  const ASTEROID_MID_R = 22;
  const ASTEROID_LIL_R = 12;
  const ASTEROID_SPEED_RANGE = [40, 110];
  const SAFE_RADIUS_AT_SPAWN = 160; // no asteroid spawns inside this disk

  const SCORE_BIG = 20, SCORE_MID = 50, SCORE_LIL = 100;

  // === STATE ==============================================================
  const canvas = document.getElementById("game");
  const ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");
  const restartBtn = document.getElementById("restart");

  let ship;        // { x, y, vx, vy, angle, alive }
  let bullets;     // [{ x, y, vx, vy, life }]
  let asteroids;   // [{ x, y, vx, vy, r, shape: number[] }]
  let score;
  let highScore = 0;
  let phase;       // "playing" | "gameover"
  let fireCooldown;
  let keys;
  let lastTime;
  let loopId = null;
  let audioCtx = null;

  function reset() {
    ship = { x: W / 2, y: H / 2, vx: 0, vy: 0, angle: -Math.PI / 2, alive: true };
    bullets = [];
    asteroids = [];
    spawnWave(5);
    score = 0;
    phase = "playing";
    fireCooldown = 0;
    keys = new Set();
    lastTime = performance.now();
    publishState();
    startLoop();
  }

  function startLoop() {
    if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
    function tick(now) {
      try {
        const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
        lastTime = now;
        update(dt);
        render();
        publishState();
      } catch (err) {
        console.error("loop error", err);
      }
      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }

  function spawnWave(n) {
    for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) spawnAsteroid(ASTEROID_BIG_R);
  }

  function spawnAsteroid(r) {
    let x, y, tries = 0;
    do {
      x = Math.random() * W;
      y = Math.random() * H;
      tries++;
    } while (Math.hypot(x - ship.x, y - ship.y) < SAFE_RADIUS_AT_SPAWN && tries < 30);
    const angle = Math.random() * Math.PI * 2;
    const speed = ASTEROID_SPEED_RANGE[0] + Math.random() * (ASTEROID_SPEED_RANGE[1] - ASTEROID_SPEED_RANGE[0]);
    asteroids.push({
      x, y,
      vx: Math.cos(angle) * speed,
      vy: Math.sin(angle) * speed,
      r,
      shape: makeAsteroidShape(),
    });
  }

  function makeAsteroidShape() {
    // Per-vertex radius multipliers, 8-12 vertices for a knobbly silhouette.
    const n = 8 + Math.floor(Math.random() * 5);
    const out = [];
    for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) out.push(0.75 + Math.random() * 0.45);
    return out;
  }

  // === UPDATE =============================================================
  function update(dt) {
    if (phase !== "playing") return;

    // Ship.
    if (ship.alive) {
      const turnIn = (keys.has("ArrowRight") || keys.has("d") || keys.has("D") ? 1 : 0)
                   - (keys.has("ArrowLeft")  || keys.has("a") || keys.has("A") ? 1 : 0);
      ship.angle += turnIn * SHIP_TURN * dt;

      const thrusting = keys.has("ArrowUp") || keys.has("w") || keys.has("W");
      if (thrusting) {
        ship.vx += Math.cos(ship.angle) * SHIP_THRUST * dt;
        ship.vy += Math.sin(ship.angle) * SHIP_THRUST * dt;
      }
      // Mild friction so the ship doesn't drift forever.
      const decay = Math.pow(SHIP_FRICTION, dt);
      ship.vx *= decay;
      ship.vy *= decay;
      ship.x = wrap(ship.x + ship.vx * dt, W);
      ship.y = wrap(ship.y + ship.vy * dt, H);

      fireCooldown -= dt;
      if ((keys.has(" ") || keys.has("Space")) && fireCooldown <= 0) {
        fire();
        fireCooldown = FIRE_COOLDOWN;
      }
    }

    // Bullets.
    for (const b of bullets) {
      b.life -= dt;
      b.x = wrap(b.x + b.vx * dt, W);
      b.y = wrap(b.y + b.vy * dt, H);
    }
    bullets = bullets.filter((b) => b.life > 0);

    // Asteroids.
    for (const a of asteroids) {
      a.x = wrap(a.x + a.vx * dt, W);
      a.y = wrap(a.y + a.vy * dt, H);
    }

    // Bullet-asteroid collisions.
    for (let i = asteroids.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
      const a = asteroids[i];
      let hit = false;
      for (let j = bullets.length - 1; j >= 0; j--) {
        const b = bullets[j];
        if (Math.hypot(a.x - b.x, a.y - b.y) < a.r) {
          bullets.splice(j, 1);
          hit = true;
          break;
        }
      }
      if (hit) splitAsteroid(i);
    }

    // Ship-asteroid collisions.
    if (ship.alive) {
      for (const a of asteroids) {
        if (Math.hypot(a.x - ship.x, a.y - ship.y) < a.r + SHIP_RADIUS * 0.6) {
          ship.alive = false;
          phase = "gameover";
          if (score > highScore) highScore = score;
          beep(110, 0.3);
          break;
        }
      }
    }

    // Wave clear -> spawn next.
    if (asteroids.length === 0 && phase === "playing") {
      spawnWave(5);
    }
  }

  function fire() {
    bullets.push({
      x: ship.x + Math.cos(ship.angle) * SHIP_RADIUS,
      y: ship.y + Math.sin(ship.angle) * SHIP_RADIUS,
      vx: ship.vx + Math.cos(ship.angle) * BULLET_SPEED,
      vy: ship.vy + Math.sin(ship.angle) * BULLET_SPEED,
      life: BULLET_LIFE,
    });
    beep(820, 0.04);
  }

  function splitAsteroid(idx) {
    const a = asteroids[idx];
    asteroids.splice(idx, 1);
    if (a.r === ASTEROID_BIG_R) {
      score += SCORE_BIG;
      for (let i = 0; i < 2; i++) addChild(a, ASTEROID_MID_R);
      beep(440, 0.06);
    } else if (a.r === ASTEROID_MID_R) {
      score += SCORE_MID;
      for (let i = 0; i < 2; i++) addChild(a, ASTEROID_LIL_R);
      beep(560, 0.05);
    } else {
      score += SCORE_LIL;
      beep(700, 0.05);
    }
  }

  function addChild(parent, r) {
    const angle = Math.random() * Math.PI * 2;
    const speed = ASTEROID_SPEED_RANGE[0] + Math.random() * (ASTEROID_SPEED_RANGE[1] - ASTEROID_SPEED_RANGE[0]);
    asteroids.push({
      x: parent.x, y: parent.y,
      vx: Math.cos(angle) * speed,
      vy: Math.sin(angle) * speed,
      r,
      shape: makeAsteroidShape(),
    });
  }

  // === RENDER =============================================================
  function render() {
    ctx.fillStyle = "#05050c";
    ctx.fillRect(0, 0, W, H);

    // Asteroids.
    ctx.strokeStyle = "#a5a5b8";
    ctx.lineWidth = 1.5;
    for (const a of asteroids) drawAsteroid(a);

    // Bullets.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#ffd23f";
    for (const b of bullets) {
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.arc(b.x, b.y, 2.4, 0, Math.PI * 2);
      ctx.fill();
    }

    // Ship.
    if (ship.alive) drawShip();

    // HUD.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
    ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
    ctx.textAlign = "left";
    ctx.fillText(`Score ${score}`, 12, 22);
    ctx.textAlign = "right";
    ctx.fillText(`Best ${highScore}`, W - 12, 22);

    if (phase === "gameover") {
      ctx.fillStyle = "rgba(5,5,12,0.65)";
      ctx.fillRect(0, 0, W, H);
      ctx.textAlign = "center";
      ctx.font = "44px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillStyle = "#ff52ae";
      ctx.fillText("Game Over", W / 2, H / 2 - 20);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
      ctx.font = "20px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText(`Final score ${score}`, W / 2, H / 2 + 14);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#a5a5b8";
      ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText("Press R or click Restart", W / 2, H / 2 + 42);
    }
  }

  function drawShip() {
    ctx.save();
    ctx.translate(ship.x, ship.y);
    ctx.rotate(ship.angle);
    ctx.strokeStyle = "#7df9ff";
    ctx.lineWidth = 2;
    ctx.beginPath();
    ctx.moveTo(SHIP_RADIUS, 0);
    ctx.lineTo(-SHIP_RADIUS * 0.9, SHIP_RADIUS * 0.7);
    ctx.lineTo(-SHIP_RADIUS * 0.5, 0);
    ctx.lineTo(-SHIP_RADIUS * 0.9, -SHIP_RADIUS * 0.7);
    ctx.closePath();
    ctx.stroke();
    if (keys && (keys.has("ArrowUp") || keys.has("w") || keys.has("W"))) {
      ctx.strokeStyle = "#ffd23f";
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.moveTo(-SHIP_RADIUS * 0.5, 0);
      ctx.lineTo(-SHIP_RADIUS - 6 - Math.random() * 4, 0);
      ctx.stroke();
    }
    ctx.restore();
  }

  function drawAsteroid(a) {
    ctx.beginPath();
    for (let i = 0; i < a.shape.length; i++) {
      const t = (i / a.shape.length) * Math.PI * 2;
      const r = a.r * a.shape[i];
      const x = a.x + Math.cos(t) * r;
      const y = a.y + Math.sin(t) * r;
      if (i === 0) ctx.moveTo(x, y); else ctx.lineTo(x, y);
    }
    ctx.closePath();
    ctx.stroke();
  }

  // === INPUT ==============================================================
  document.addEventListener("keydown", (ev) => {
    if (ev.key === "r" || ev.key === "R") { reset(); return; }
    keys.add(ev.key);
    if (ev.key === " ") ev.preventDefault();
  });
  document.addEventListener("keyup", (ev) => { keys.delete(ev.key); });
  restartBtn.addEventListener("click", reset);

  // === AUDIO (lazy) =======================================================
  function beep(freq, durSec) {
    try {
      if (!audioCtx) audioCtx = new (window.AudioContext || window.webkitAudioContext)();
      const osc = audioCtx.createOscillator();
      const gain = audioCtx.createGain();
      osc.type = "triangle";
      osc.frequency.value = freq;
      gain.gain.setValueAtTime(0.04, audioCtx.currentTime);
      gain.gain.exponentialRampToValueAtTime(0.001, audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
      osc.connect(gain); gain.connect(audioCtx.destination);
      osc.start(); osc.stop(audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
    } catch {}
  }

  // === OBSERVABLE CONTRACT ===============================================
  function publishState() {
    window.__game = {
      state: phase,                                  // "playing" | "gameover"
      score,
      player: {
        x: ship.x,
        y: ship.y,
        // wrap() keeps coords in [0, W) and [0, H), so visible is always true
        // — but assert it explicitly so the contract is honest.
        visible: ship.x >= 0 && ship.x < W && ship.y >= 0 && ship.y < H,
      },
      objective: "Shoot the asteroids before they hit your ship. Big rocks split into smaller ones.",
    };
  }

  // === HELPERS ============================================================
  function wrap(v, max) {
    if (v < 0) return v + max;
    if (v >= max) return v - max;
    return v;
  }

  // === BOOT ===============================================================
  reset();
  </script>
</body>
</html>
```

Clicking it must fully reset the game (same code path as the R key). It must be visible and clickable at all times — not only on game-over.\n72: - The `<header>`'s `<h1>` text must match `meta.json.title` exactly.\n73: - The `<p class=\"game-description\">` text must match `meta.json.description` exactly.\n74: - The `<li>` items in `<ul class=\"game-controls\">` must match `meta.json.controls` in order.\n75: \n76: ### Behavior\n77: - The game must start rendering immediately on page load (no \"click to start\" required).\n78: - Use keyboard and/or mouse input.\n79: - The script must not throw any uncaught exception in the first 5 seconds.\n80: - If you use `localStorage` or `sessionStorage`, all keys must start with the prefix `slop-factory:orbit-radius:`.\n81: \n82: ### Style\n83: - Dark background (the gallery is dark — recommend `#0a0a0f` or similar).\n84: - High-contrast neon accents (e.g. cyan `#7df9ff`, magenta `#ff00aa`, yellow `#ffd23f`).\n85: - The `<header>` is shown above the canvas — keep its styling minimal and let the canvas content shine.\n86: \n87: ## What makes a tiny canvas game feel good\n88: \n89: Aim for these qualities in every game you produce:\n90: \n91: 1. **Immediate input response** — every keystroke or click produces visible action within one frame. Never make the player wait.\n92: 2. **Clear feedback** — the player always knows when something happened. Use color flashes, particles, brief screen shake, score popups, or sound on every meaningful event (hit, score, miss, death).\n93: 3. **Win/lose state** — the game has a clear end condition. On lose, draw \"Game Over\" + final score + restart instructions on the canvas. On win, celebrate (color burst, \"You Win!\" overlay).\n94: 4. **Restart without page reload** — both pressing R AND clicking the `<button id=\"restart\">` reset the game cleanly via the same code path. Mention both in `controls` (e.g. \"Press R or click Restart\"). On restart you MUST `cancelAnimationFrame` any pending loop before starting a new one (otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game eventually slows to a crawl).\n95: 5. **Progressive difficulty** — start gentle so the player can learn, then ramp up. Don't dump them into chaos at t=0.\n96: 6. **No dead time** — something interesting on screen at all times. Idle background animation (stars, gradient pulse, particles) is fine.\n97: 7. **Persistent score / progress UI** — drawn on the canvas at the top corner, always visible.\n98: 8. **Audio (optional but encouraged)** — generate tones with `OscillatorNode` (no external assets). **Do not auto-start audio on page load** — browsers block this and it will throw. Initialize the `AudioContext` lazily on the first keypress or click. Wrap audio code in try/catch.\n99: \n100: ## Playable Contract — non-negotiable\n101: \n102: These are not aspirations. A game that violates any of these is broken even if it runs without errors. After your implementation finishes, the validator loads the page in a real browser and runs an 8-second playtest with synthetic input — failures here are reported back to you and must be fixed before the game ships.\n103: \n104: ### 1. The player can lose\n105: \n106: There must be a collision check (or other end condition) that fires `gameOver` and stops the active gameplay loop. \"Game runs forever, no death\" is broken. Examples that have failed in the past: collision check compared a value against itself; \"spike\" collision only clamped position instead of killing the player; obstacles never reached the player.\n107: \n108: ### 2. The player can score\n109: \n110: The score must increase on a **discrete event** (a survived obstacle, a collected pickup, an enemy destroyed, a beat hit). \"Score = elapsed seconds\" is allowed but weak. Score must be drawn on the canvas at all times.\n111: \n112: ### 3. The first 3 seconds are survivable with no input\n113: \n114: Define a `SAFE_RADIUS` (e.g. 200px) around the player's starting position. When spawning hazards, **reject any spawn that is within `SAFE_RADIUS` of the player AND would reach them within 3 seconds at current hazard speed**. Pre-spawning hazards on top of the player at t=0 has caused frame-1 deaths in past games. Don't do it.\n115: \n116: ### 4. The control actually changes the outcome\n117: \n118: Whatever the player presses must, in the next frame, change a value that affects collision or score. If a control is purely cosmetic (e.g. \"stretch horizontally\" but the gaps are vertical), the game is fundamentally broken even if it runs. After writing, **re-read the game's concept and trace one frame**: does the input do what the concept claims?\n119: \n120: ### 5. dt-based physics, not per-frame constants\n121: \n122: All movement and timing must scale by delta time (seconds since last frame). Per-frame constants (`x += 5` every frame) make games run 2.4× faster on a 144Hz display.\n123: \n124: ```js\n125: let lastTime = performance.now();\n126: function loop(now) {\n127:   const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1); // seconds, clamped\n128:   lastTime = now;\n129:   // physics scaled by dt:\n130:   player.x += player.vx * dt;\n131:   enemy.y  += enemy.vy  * dt;\n132:   spawnTimer -= dt;\n133:   // ...\n134:   requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n135: }\n136: requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n137: ```\n138: \n139: ### 6. Restart fully resets AND cancels the prior loop\n140: \n141: Both R and the Restart button must call the same reset function. That function MUST `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` the previous loop before scheduling a new one — otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game slows to a crawl over time.\n142: \n143: ```js\n144: let loopId = null;\n145: function startLoop() {\n146:   if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);\n147:   function tick(now) { /* ... */ loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick); }\n148:   loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);\n149: }\n150: ```\n151: \n152: ### 7. Game-over overlay shows score and restart instructions\n153: \n154: When the player dies, draw on the canvas: \"Game Over\", final score, current high score (if you persist one), and \"Press R or click Restart\". Do not rely on the HTML restart button alone — players need to know they can restart.\n155: \n156: ## Observable State Contract — required for playtest\n157: \n158: In addition to the rules above, your game **must** expose its current state on `window.__game` so the automated playtest can observe what the player would observe. A game that does not expose this object is rejected.\n159: \n160: ```js\n161: window.__game = {\n162:   state: \"menu\",        // \"menu\" | \"playing\" | \"gameover\" — exact strings, no others\n163:   score: 0,             // current score, number\n164:   player: {\n165:     x: 400,             // player x in canvas coordinates (0 = left)\n166:     y: 300,             // player y in canvas coordinates (0 = top)\n167:     visible: true,      // true if the player is currently rendered inside the canvas viewport\n168:   },\n169:   objective: \"Dodge falling neon blocks. Survive as long as possible.\", // 10-200 chars, one sentence\n170: };\n171: ```\n172: \n173: Rules:\n174: - The object must exist from the very first frame, before any input.\n175: - Update `state`, `score`, `player.x`, `player.y`, and `player.visible` whenever they change. The simplest approach is to update them at the end of your render/update step each frame.\n176: - `state` must only ever be one of the three exact strings. No other values.\n177: - `player.visible` is critical. **If your camera follows the player, the camera math must keep `player.visible` = true at all times.** A camera that lags the player off-screen is the most common failure here.\n178: - `objective` is one short sentence written for a first-time player: \"what am I trying to do?\" — derivable from observing the game alone, not from reading the meta.\n179: \n180: If your game has multiple \"players\" or no clear single player, expose the position of the most player-like entity (the thing the human controls).\n181: \n182: ## What the validator checks\n183: \n184: Your output is checked programmatically. // Constants
const W = 800, H = 600;

// === STATE ==============================================================
const canvas = document.getElementById("game");
const ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");

let keys = new Set();

// === RESET ==============================================================
function reset() {
  player = { angle: 0, radius: ORBIT_INNER_R, alive: true };
  hazards = [];
  score = 0;
  phase = "playing";
  keys = new Set();
  lastTime = performance.now();
  publishState();
  startLoop();
}

// === LOOP / UPDATE ======================================================
function startLoop() {
  if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
  function tick(now) {
    try {
      const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
      lastTime = now;

      // Update player rotation based on input
      // For now placeholder: rotate constant
      // We'll just increment angle
      player.angle += Math.PI * dt * 0.5; // rotate at 0.5 rev/s

      // TODO: handle orbit toggle via click/key

      // Clear canvas
      ctx.clearRect(0, 0, W, H);

      // Render player
      const x = W/2 + Math.cos(player.angle) * player.radius;
      const y = H/2 + Math.sin(player.angle) * player.radius;
      ctx.fillStyle = "#7df9ff";
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.arc(x, y, PLAYER_RADIUS, 0, Math.PI * 2);
      ctx.fill();

      // Publish state
      publishState();
    } catch (err) {
      console.error("loop error", err);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }
  loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
}

// === INPUT ==============================================================
document.addEventListener("keydown", (ev) => {
  if (ev.key === "r" || ev.key === "R") { reset(); return; }
  if (ev.key === " " || ev.key === "Enter") { /* toggle radius */ }
  keys.add(ev.key);
});
document.addEventListener("keyup", (ev) => { keys.delete(ev.key); });
restartBtn.addEventListener("click", reset);

// === AUDIO (lazy) =======================================================
function beep(freq, durSec) {
  try {
    if (!audioCtx) audioCtx = new (window.AudioContext || window.webkitAudioContext)();
    const osc = audioCtx.createOscillator();
    const gain = audioCtx.createGain();
    osc.type = "triangle";
    osc.frequency.value = freq;
    gain.gain.setValueAtTime(0.04, audioCtx.currentTime);
    gain.gain.exponentialRampToValueAtTime(0.001, audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
    osc.connect(gain);
    gain.connect(audioCtx.destination);
    osc.start();
    osc.stop(audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
  } catch {}
}

// === OBSERVABLE CONTRACT ================================================
function publishState() {
  window.__game = {
    state: phase,
    score,
    player: {
      x: W/2 + Math.cos(player.angle) * player.radius,
      y: H/2 + Math.sin(player.angle) * player.radius,
      visible: true,
    },
    objective: "Toggle orbit radius to avoid obstacles.",
  };
}

// === BOOT ================================================================
reset();
```

// === CONFIG =============================================================
const W = 800, H = 600;

// === STATE ==============================================================
const canvas = document.getElementById('game');
const ctx = canvas.getContext('2d');
let player = { x: W/2, y: H/2, radius: 5 };
let hazards = []; // obstacles
let score = 0;
let phase = 'playing'; // or 'menu'?
let lastTime = performance.now();
let loopId = null;

// === LOOP ==============================================================
function startLoop() {
  if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
  function tick(now) {
    try {
      const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
      lastTime = now;
      // update logic (none for now)
      // clear canvas
      ctx.clearRect(0, 0, W, H);
      // render player
      ctx.fillStyle = '#7df9ff';
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.arc(player.x, player.y, player.radius, 0, Math.PI * 2);
      ctx.fill();
    } catch (e) {
      console.error('loop error', e);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }
  loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
}

// === BOOT ==============================================================
startLoop();

Implementation:

- Player always moves at angular speed `playerAngularSpeed = Math.PI * 1.5` rad/s (approx 1.5π rad/s). The player's current orbit radius = either innerRadius (80) or outerRadius (200).

- Hazards are objects with:
    - angle: initial random angle
    - radius: either innerRadius or outerRadius (same as the orbit radius)
    - angularSpeed: maybe `playerAngularSpeed * 0.6` (slower) or varied; we can random.
    - r: visual radius (size) maybe 8.
- Each tick, hazard.angle += hazard.angularSpeed * dt.
- Then if hazard's radius == player.radius, compute angular distance diff = Math.abs(wrapAngle difference). If diff less than some threshold (like 0.2 rad) when they are at same radius, then collision -> gameover.

Better: For collision detection, we can compute Euclidean distance: hazard.x = cx + hazard.radius * cos(hazard.angle); hazard.y = cy + hazard.radius * sin(hazard.angle); compute distance between hazard and player; if < (hazard.r + player.r) => collision.

- Score: increments for each hazard that passes (i.e., if hazard gives a full circle pass and then disappears). We can spawn hazards periodically and have them orbit. When hazard makes a full rotation past a certain angle threshold relative to start, we can count as scored. Simpler: we remove hazard after a certain time (like after 8 seconds) and increment score when removed.

Collision detection will happen each frame: compute distance as above. If collision and phase is "playing", set player.alive false (or just set phase to "gameover").

Read CONVENTIONS.md and DESIGN.md in your working directory before doing anything else.

Your job in this stage is to break the implementation into a numbered list of small, ordered tasks. The next stage will work through this list one task at a time, in a single LLM session, reloading the page after each task and capturing console errors for you to fix.

Title: Orbit Radius
Concept: The dot constantly orbits a central point; each tap toggles between a tight inner orbit and a wide outer orbit, letting you navigate obstacles placed at either radius.

**You must produce one output file: `TASKS.md` in the working directory.** Use your write/edit tool to create this file on disk. After your turn ends, the validator runs `readFile("TASKS.md")` — if the file does not exist on disk, validation fails and your work is wasted. **Do NOT reply with the task list as inline chat text.** The list must be written to the file.

**Tool calls MUST be emitted as actual function/tool invocations — NOT described or simulated inside your reasoning/analysis text.** Writing tool-shaped JSON like `{"filePath":"TASKS.md","content":"..."}` *inside your chain-of-thought* does NOT invoke any tool. It is just text in the analysis channel that nothing reads or executes. The same goes for `{"pattern":"**/*"}` etc. — those only do something when emitted as a real `tool_use` event from the action channel, never when written into your thinking. Several prior runs have failed by hallucinating tool calls inside reasoning; do not repeat that pattern. If at any point you "describe" a tool call in your thinking instead of invoking it, you must immediately invoke the real tool afterward. The format MUST be a numbered list, one task per line, each line starting with `<number>.`:

```
1. <Task title> — <one-sentence description of what to do>
2. <Task title> — <one-sentence description>
...
```

The next stage parses tasks by matching `^\d+\.` at the start of each line. Don't use bullet sub-lists, don't number sub-tasks (1.1, 1.2). One number, one task, one line.

### What makes a good task

- **Small enough** to complete in a single coding turn (~50–150 lines of edits).
- **Testable**: after this task, what does the page do that it didn't before?
- **Leaves the file runnable**: at the end of the task, no syntax errors, no uncaught exceptions on load. Even if the game isn't playable yet, it must load.
- **Specific**: don't say "implement player". Say "implement player movement: arrow keys set keys.left/right; in update(), apply acceleration in the pressed direction with cap at MAX_SPEED, friction otherwise; clamp x to canvas bounds."
- **Concrete numbers**: this is where pixel sizes, ms intervals, and px/sec speeds live. Pick them. The implement stage will use them verbatim.

### Required coverage (in roughly this order)

Cover at minimum, ordered as below. Add tasks if the design demands more (powerups, level progression, screen wrap, etc):

1. **HTML scaffold + meta.json**: header (h1, description, controls list), canvas, restart button per CONVENTIONS.md. Stub `<script>` block (empty). meta.json matching header text.
2. **Game state objects**: player, hazards, score, phase using plain object literals at the top of the script. Set initial values.
3. **requestAnimationFrame loop**: startLoop() with `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` discipline at the top. Body wrapped in try/catch. Calls update(dt) and render(). Don't draw anything yet besides clearing the canvas.
4. **Input**: a `keys` Set populated by document keydown/keyup. R-key restart hook. Restart button click hook. Both call the same reset() function.
5. **Player movement**: dt-based physics tied to the key inputs from task 4. Verify each control listed in meta.json actually changes a value.
6. **Hazards**: data structure, spawn rules with a SAFE_RADIUS so nothing spawns on top of the player at t=0, motion (dt-scaled).
7. **Collision detection**: player vs hazards. On hit, transition phase to "gameover" and stop the active gameplay.
8. **Score**: discrete events that increase score, drawn on canvas at top-left.
9. **Render**: clear canvas, draw background, hazards, player, HUD. Player must be the highest-contrast object on screen.
10. **Game-over overlay**: dim background, "Game Over", final score, "Press R or click Restart".
11. **Observable contract**: populate `window.__game = { state, score, player:{x,y,visible}, objective }` every frame. `state` is "playing" | "gameover". `objective` is a 10–200 char single-sentence description.
12. **Audio**: lazy-init AudioContext inside a beep() helper, wrapped in try/catch. Hook into hit / score / death events.

You may merge two trivial tasks if they truly belong together (e.g. tasks 1+2 if state is just three lines). You may split a complex task in two. Aim for 10–15 tasks total.
